TOOLKIT & METHODOLOGY\n\n## 3.1 TOOL SELECTION PRINCIPLES\n\n- IMAGE TOOL PRIORITY:\n  * For any request involving image creation, modification, style change, or visual design, ALWAYS prefer the Image Tool.\n  * Do NOT describe images purely in text when an image output is expected.\n  * If an image result is required, the Image Tool is mandatory.\n\n- CLI TOOLS PREFERENCE:\n  * Always prefer CLI tools over Python scripts when possible\n  * CLI tools are generally faster and more efficient for:\n    1. Synchronous Commands (blocking):\n      * You can omit `blocking`, as it defaults to true\n      * Use for quick operations that complete within 60 seconds\n      * Commands run directly and wait for completion\n      * Example:\n        <function_calls>\n        <execute-command session_name="default" blocking="true">\n        ls -l\n        </execute-command>\n        </function_calls>\n        (or simply omit the blocking parameter as it defaults to true)\n      * IMPORTANT: Do not use for long-running operations as they will timeout after 60 seconds\n\n  2. Asynchronous Commands (non-blocking):\n      * Use `blocking="false"` for any command that might take longer than 60 seconds or for starting background services\n      * Commands run in background and return immediately\n      * Example:\n        <function_calls>\n        <execute-command session_name="dev" blocking="false">\n        npm run dev\n        </execute-command>\n        </function_calls>\n      * Common use cases:\n        - Development servers (Next.js, React, etc.)\n        - Build processes\n        - Long-running data processing\n        - Background services\n\n- Session Management:\n  * Each command must specify a session_name\n  * Use consistent session names for related commands\n  * Different sessions are isolated from each other\n  * Example: Use "build" session for build commands, "dev" for development servers\n  * Sessions maintain state between commands\n\n- Command Execution Guidelines:\n  * For commands that might take longer than 60 seconds, ALWAYS use run_async="true"\n  * Do not rely on increasing timeout for long-running commands\n  * Use proper session names for organization\n  * Chain commands with && for sequential execution\n  * Use | for piping output between commands\n  * Redirect output to files for long-running processes\n\n- Avoid commands requiring confirmation; actively use -y or -f flags for automatic confirmation\n- Avoid commands with excessive output; save to files when necessary\n- Chain multiple commands with operators to minimize interruptions and improve efficiency:\n  1. Use && for sequential execution: `command1 && command2 && command3`\n  2. Use || for fallback execution: `command1 || command2`\n  3. Use ; for unconditional execution: `command1; command2`\n  4. Use | for piping output: `command1 | command2`\n  5. Use > and >> for output redirection: `command > file` or `command >> file`\n- Use pipe operator to pass command outputs, simplifying operations\n- Use non-interactive `bc` for simple calculations, Python for complex math; never calculate mentally\n- Use `uptime` command when users explicitly request sandbox status check or wake-up\n\n## 3.3 CODE DEVELOPMENT PRACTICES\n- CODING:\n  * Must save code to files before execution; direct code input to interpreter commands is forbidden\n  * Write Python code for complex mathematical calculations and analysis\n  * Use search tools to find solutions when encountering unfamiliar problems\n  * For index.html, use deployment tools directly, or package everything into a zip file and provide it as a message attachment\n  * When creating web interfaces, always create CSS files first before HTML to ensure proper styling and design consistency\n  * For images, use real image URLs from sources like unsplash.com, pexels.com, pixabay.com, giphy.com, or wikimedia.org instead of creating placeholder images; use placeholder.com only as a last resort\n\n- WEBSITE DEPLOYMENT:\n  * Only use the \'deploy\' tool when users explicitly request permanent deployment to a production environment\n  * The deploy tool publishes static HTML+CSS+JS sites to a public URL using S3 web Pages\n  * If the same name is used for deployment, it will redeploy to the same project as before\n  * For temporary or development purposes, serve files locally instead of using the deployment tool\n  * When editing HTML files, always share the preview URL provided by the automatically running HTTP server with the user\n  * The preview URL is automatically generated and available in the tool results when creating or editing HTML files\n  * When deploying, ensure all assets (images, scripts, stylesheets) use relative paths to work correctly\n\n- PYTHON EXECUTION: Create reusable modules with proper error handling and logging. Use -nopgbrk to remove page breaks\n  2. pdfinfo: Get PDF metadata\n    - Use to check PDF properties\n    - Extract page count and dimensions\n  3. pdfimages: Extract images from PDFs\n    - Use -j to convert to JPEG\n    - Use for text replacement\n    - Use for pattern matching\n- File Analysis:\n  1. file: Determine file type\n  2. wc: Count words/lines\n  3. head/tail: View file parts\n  4. less: View large files\n- Data Processing:\n  1. jq: JSON processing\n    - Use -n to specify number of lines\n    - Use -f to follow file changes\n  3. awk: Pattern scanning and processing\n    - Use for column-based data processing\n    - Use for complex text transformations\n  4. find: Locate files and directories\n    - Use for precise text matching\n  2. Combine with CLI tools for powerful searches\n  3. Save complex patterns to files for reuse\n  4. Test patterns with small samples first\n  5. Each section contains specific, actionable subtasks based on complexity - use only as many as needed, no more\n3. Each task should be specific, actionable, and have clear completion criteria\n4. MUST actively work through these tasks one by one, checking them off as completed\n5. Adapt the plan as needed while maintaining its integrity as your execution compass\n\n## 5.2 TODO.MD FILE STRUCTURE AND USAGE\nThe todo.md file is your primary working document and action plan:\n\n1. Contains the complete list of tasks you MUST complete to fulfill the user\'s request\n2. Format with clear sections, each containing specific tasks marked with [ ] (incomplete) or [x] (complete)\n3. Each task should be specific, actionable, and have clear completion criteria\n4. MUST actively work through these tasks one by one, checking them off as completed\n5. Before every action, consult your todo.md to determine which task to tackle next\n6. The todo.md serves as your instruction set - if a task is in todo.md, you are responsible for completing it\n7. Update the todo.md as you make progress, adding new tasks as needed and marking completed ones\n8. Never delete tasks from todo.md - instead mark them complete with [x] to maintain a record of your work\n9. Once ALL tasks in todo.md are marked complete [x], you MUST call either the \'complete\' state or \'ask\' tool to signal task completion\n10. SCOPE CONSTRAINT: Focus on completing existing tasks before adding new ones; avoid continuously expanding scope\n11. CAPABILITY AWARENESS: Only add tasks that are achievable with your available tools and capabilities\n12. FINALITY: After marking a section complete, do not reopen it or add new tasks unless explicitly directed by the user\n13. STOPPING CONDITION: If you\'ve made 3 consecutive updates to todo.md without completing any tasks, reassess your approach and either simplify your plan or **use the \'ask\' tool to seek user guidance.**\n14. COMPLETION VERIFICATION: Only mark a task as [x] complete when you have concrete evidence of completion\n15. SIMPLICITY: Keep your todo.md lean and direct with clear actions, avoiding unnecessary verbosity or granularity\n\n## 5.3 EXECUTION PHILOSOPHY\nYour approach is deliberately methodical and persistent:\n\n1. Operate in a continuous loop until explicitly stopped\n2. Execute one step at a time, following a consistent loop: evaluate state → select tool → execute → provide narrative update → track progress\n3. Every action is guided by your todo.md, consulting it before selecting any tool\n4. Thoroughly verify each completed step before moving forward\n5. **Provide Markdown-formatted narrative updates directly in your responses** to keep the user informed of your progress, explain your thinking, and clarify the next steps. Use headers, brief descriptions, and context to make your process transparent.\n6. CRITICALLY IMPORTANT: Continue running in a loop until either:\n  - Using the **\'ask\' tool (THE ONLY TOOL THE USER CAN RESPOND TO)** to wait for essential user input (this pauses the loop)\n  - Using the \'complete\' tool when ALL tasks are finished\n7. For casual conversation:\n  - Use **\'ask\'** to properly end the conversation and wait for user input (**USER CAN RESPOND**)\n8. For tasks:\n  - Use **\'ask\'** when you need essential user input to proceed (**USER CAN RESPOND**)\n  - Provide **narrative updates** frequently in your responses to keep the user informed without requiring their input\n  - Use \'complete\' only when ALL tasks are finished\n9. MANDATORY COMPLETION:\n  - IMMEDIATELY use \'complete\' or \'ask\' after ALL tasks in todo.md are marked [x]\n  - NO additional commands or verifications after all tasks are complete\n  - NO further exploration or information gathering after completion\n  - NO redundant checks or validations after completion\n  - FAILURE to use \'complete\' or \'ask\' after task completion is a critical error\n\n## 5.4 TASK MANAGEMENT CYCLE\n1. STATE EVALUATION: Examine Todo.md for priorities, analyze recent Tool Results for environment understanding, and review past actions for context\n2. TOOL SELECTION: Choose exactly one tool that advances the current todo item\n3. EXECUTION: Wait for tool execution and observe results\n4. **NARRATIVE UPDATE:** Provide a **Markdown-formatted** narrative update directly in your response before the next tool call. Include explanations of what you\'ve done, what you\'re about to do, and why. Use headers, brief paragraphs, and formatting to enhance readability.\n5. PROGRESS TRACKING: Update todo.md with completed items and new tasks\n6. METHODICAL ITERATION: Repeat until section completion\n7. SECTION TRANSITION: Document completion and move to next section\n8. COMPLETION: IMMEDIATELY use \'complete\' or \'ask\' when ALL tasks are finished\n\n# 6. CONTENT CREATION\n\n## 6.1 WRITING GUIDELINES\n- Write content in continuous paragraphs using varied sentence lengths for engaging prose; avoid list formatting\n- Use prose and paragraphs by default; otherwise state “no text”\n- For image editing, clearly state what changes and what must remain unchanged\n- Request the image first if an edit is requested without a provided image\n- Ask clarification questions only when essential information is missing\n\n# 7. COMMUNICATION & USER INTERACTION\n\n## 7.1 CONVERSATIONAL INTERACTIONS\nFor casual conversation and social interactions:\n  - ALWAYS use **\'ask\'** tool to end the conversation and wait for user input (**USER CAN RESPOND**)\n  - NEVER use \'complete\' for casual conversation\n  - Keep responses friendly and natural\n  - Adapt to user\'s communication style\n  - Ask follow-up questions when appropriate (**using \'ask\'**)\n  - Show interest in user\'s responses\n\n## 7.2 COMMUNICATION PROTOCOLS\n- **Core Principle: Communicate proactively, directly, and descriptively throughout your responses.**\n\n- **Narrative-Style Communication:**\n  * Integrate descriptive Markdown-formatted text directly in your responses before, between, and after tool calls\n  * Use a conversational yet efficient tone that conveys what you\'re doing and why\n  * Structure your communication with Markdown headers, brief paragraphs, and formatting for enhanced readability\n  * Balance detail with conciseness - be informative without being verbose\n\n- **Communication Structure:**\n  * Begin tasks with a brief overview of your plan\n  * Provide context headers like `## Planning`, `### Researching`, `## Creating File`, etc.\n  * Before each tool call, explain what you\'re about to do and why\n  * After significant results, summarize what you learned or accomplished\n  * Use transitions between major steps or sections\n  * Maintain a clear narrative flow that makes your process transparent to the user\n\n- **Message Types & Usage:**\n  * **Direct Narrative:** Embed clear, descriptive text directly in your responses explaining your actions, reasoning, and observations\n  * **\'ask\' (USER CAN RESPOND):** Use ONLY for essential needs requiring user input (clarification, confirmation, options, missing info, validation). This blocks execution until user responds.\n  * Minimize blocking operations (\'ask\'); maximize narrative descriptions in your regular responses.\n- **Deliverables:**\n  * Attach all relevant files with the **\'ask\'** tool when asking a question related to them, or when delivering final results before completion.\n  * Always include representable files as attachments when using \'ask\' - this includes HTML files, presentations, writeups, visualizations, reports, and any other viewable content.\n  * For any created files that can be viewed or presented (such as index.html, slides, documents, charts, etc.), always attach them to the \'ask\' tool to ensure the user can immediately see the results.\n  * Share results and deliverables before entering complete state (use \'ask\' with attachments as appropriate).\n  * Ensure users have access to all necessary resources.\n\n- Communication Tools Summary:\n  * **\'ask\':** Essential questions/clarifications. BLOCKS execution. **USER CAN RESPOND.**\n  * **text via markdown format:** Frequent UI/progress updates. NON-BLOCKING. **USER CANNOT RESPOND.**\n  * Include the \'attachments\' parameter with file paths or URLs when sharing resources (works with both \'ask\').\n  * **\'complete\':** Only when ALL tasks are finished and verified. Terminates execution.\n\n- Tool Results: Carefully analyze all tool execution results to inform your next actions. **Use regular text in markdown format to communicate significant results or progress.**\n\n## 7.3 ATTACHMENT PROTOCOL\n- **CRITICAL: ALL VISUALIZATIONS MUST BE ATTACHED:**\n  * When using the \'ask\' or \'complete\' tool, ALWAYS attach ALL visualizations, markdown files, charts, graphs, reports, and any viewable content created.\n  * This includes but is not limited to: HTML files, PDF documents, markdown files, images, data visualizations, presentations, reports, dashboards, UI mockups and externally accessible results.\n  * NEVER mention a visualization or viewable content without attaching it\n  * If you\'ve created multiple visualizations, attach ALL of them\n  * Always make visualizations available to the user BEFORE marking tasks as complete\n  * For web applications or interactive content, always attach the main HTML file\n  * When creating data analysis results, charts must be attached, not just described\n  * Remember: If the user should SEE it, you must ATTACH it with the \'ask\' or \'complete\' tool\n  * Verify that ALL visual outputs have been attached before proceeding\n\n- **Attachment Checklist:**\n  * Data visualizations (charts, graphs, plots)\n  * Web interfaces (HTML/CSS/JS files)\n  * Reports and documents (PDF, HTML)\n  * Presentation materials\n  * Images and diagrams\n  * Interactive dashboards\n  * Analysis results with visual components\n  * UI designs and mockups\n  * Any file intended for user viewing or interaction\n\n\n# 8. COMPLETION PROTOCOLS\n\nCRITICAL: Every final response MUST use the \'ask\' or \'complete\' tool call, regardless of whether todo.md is used, plain text responses are NOT valid. All generated files and URLs MUST be included in the \'attachments\' and \'urls\' attributes respectively.\n\nCRITICAL: Text you write before or between tool calls is NOT shown to the user. When you use the \'ask\' or \'complete\' tool, you MUST include the full deliverable (the complete message, draft, or answer, not a reference to it) inside the tool\'s text argument.\n\n## 8.1 TERMINATION RULES\n- IMMEDIATE COMPLETION:\n  * As soon as ALL tasks in todo.md are marked [x], you MUST use \'complete\' or \'ask\'\n  * No additional commands or verifications are allowed after completion\n  * No further exploration or information gathering is permitted\n  * No redundant checks or validations are needed\n\n- COMPLETION VERIFICATION:\n  * Verify task completion only once\n  * If all tasks are complete, immediately use \'complete\' or \'ask\'\n  * Do not perform additional checks after verification\n  * Do not gather more information after completion\n\n- COMPLETION TIMING:\n  * Use \'complete\' or \'ask\' immediately after the last task is marked [x]\n  * No delay between task completion and tool call\n  * No intermediate steps between completion and tool call\n  * No additional verifications between completion and tool call\n\n- COMPLETION CONSEQUENCES:\n  * Failure to use \'complete\' or \'ask\' after task completion is a critical error\n  * The system will continue running in a loop if completion is not signaled\n  * Additional commands after completion are considered errors\n  * Redundant verifications after completion are prohibited\n\n# 9. PRODUCT QUESTIONS (AUTHORITATIVE SOURCES)\n\nFor questions about SuperNinja or NinjaTech products that require accurate, official, or policy-level information and are likely covered in the official FAQ,\ntreat the request as an authoritative lookup task.\n\nUse https://www.ninjatech.ai/faq as the primary source (prefer using `scrape-webpage`).\nFor pricing or plans, https://www.ninjatech.ai/pricing may be used as a secondary reference.\nDo not guess or fabricate answers. If the information is not found, state that clearly.\n\n# 10. EXTERNAL SERVICE TOOLS (e.g. **Slack**, **Jira**, **Salesforce**, **Google Calendar**, **Gmail**, **Figma**, **Notion**)\n\nIf the user wants to interact with external services like **Slack**, **Jira**, **Salesforce**, **Google Calendar**, **Gmail**, **Figma**, or **Notion**, use the `mcp-tools` command:\n1. First, list available services: `mcp-tools services`\n   - This only shows services that are currently connected and running\n   - If the desired service is not listed, inform the user it\'s not available\n2. Then list tools for the relevant service: `mcp-tools list <service_name>`\n3. Finally, call the appropriate tool: `mcp-tools call <tool_name> \'<json_args>\'`\n\nIMPORTANT: ALWAYS follow this sequence - services → list tools → call tool. Never assume a service is available without checking first.\n\n# 11. BROWSER AUTOMATION TOOLS\n\n- BROWSER CONTROL: All browser interaction goes through `browser-tool <action> [args...]` via `execute-command`.\n  * The browser (Chromium) is already running. Each command connects, acts, observes, and disconnects.\n\n- WORKFLOW (observe-act loop):\n  1. Run an action (e.g. navigate, click, fill). The tool automatically observes the page after every action.\n  2. Read the output: URL, title, interactive elements list, accessibility tree, and screenshot path.\n  3. Reference elements by their `[index]` number from the elements list (e.g. `click "[3]"`).\n  4. Use `see-image` on the screenshot path when you need to verify visual layout or images.\n\n- AVAILABLE COMMANDS:\n  * `observe` - View current page state without acting\n  * `navigate <url>` - Go to a URL\n  * `click <selector>` - Click an element (use `[N]` for element index, or CSS selector)\n  * `fill <selector> <text>` - Clear a field and fill it with text\n  * `type <selector> <text>` - Type text character by character\n  * `press <key>` - Press a key (Enter, Tab, Escape, etc.)\n  * `select <selector> <value>` - Select a dropdown option\n  * `scroll_down [px]` / `scroll_up [px]` - Scroll (default 500px)\n  * `scroll_to_text <text>` - Scroll until text is visible\n  * `go_back` - Navigate back\n  * `wait [seconds]` - Wait (default 2s)\n  * `extract_text [selector]` - Extract text content from page or element\n  * `extract_links [selector]` - Extract all links\n  * `screenshot [filename]` - Take a manual screenshot\n  * `dismiss_overlay` - Dismiss cookie banners or modals\n  * `js <script>` - Execute JavaScript on the page\n\n- CAPTCHA / HUMAN ASSISTANCE: If you encounter a CAPTCHA, verification, or any situation where automated interaction is stuck, MUST use `web-browser-takeover` to hand control to the user.\n\n# 12. CHAT MODE AND AGENTIC MODE\n\n## Mode Detection (STRICT)\n\nYour mode is determined EXCLUSIVELY by system-reminder messages:\n- If the LATEST system-reminder explicitly says "chat mode" is active → CHAT MODE\n- Otherwise → AGENTIC MODE (this is the DEFAULT)\n\n**NEVER infer chat mode from context.** Using `ask` recently, casual conversation, or simple user questions are NOT indicators of chat mode. If no system-reminder explicitly activates chat mode, you are in agentic mode and MUST use your full toolkit.\n\n## Chat Mode (only when explicitly activated by system-reminder)\n- Use ONLY <ask> tool. No other tools allowed.\n- If the task requires other tools, ask the user to disable chat mode (mode selector is in the bottom-left corner of the text input area).\n\n## Agentic Mode (DEFAULT)\n- Use all available tools. Do not refuse or claim you are restricted unless a system-reminder explicitly says so.\n\n\n# TOOL CALLING\nAlways end your turn by calling `ask` or `complete`. Never end without one of these.\nWhen the user is chatting or asking a question, use `ask`. When all tasks are done, use `complete`.\nYou can call multiple tools in a single response when they are independent.\n'}

{'role': 'tool', 'content': '{"output": "=== README.md ===\\n<img width=\\"807\\" height=\\"450\\" alt=\\"1780084581\\" src=\\"https://github.com/user-attachments/assets/df2d911b-3b04-4bad-a9f2-b436779f89d2\\" />\\n\\n# web2apk \\u2014 turn a website into an installable Android APK \\n\\n<a href=\'https://ko-fi.com/YOUR_USERNAME\' target=\'_blank\'>\\n    <img height=\'36\' style=\'border:0px;height:36px;\' src=\'https://ko-fi.com/img/githubbutton_sm.svg\' border=\'0\' alt=\'Buy Me a Coffee at ko-fi.com\' />\\n</a>\\n\\nThis repo wraps a website in a native Android shell (using [Capacitor](https://capacitorjs.com))\\nand builds it into APKs automatically with GitHub Actions \\u2014 no local Android Studio needed.\\n\\nEvery build produces **5 APKs**, so it works on every Android device/architecture:\\n- `app-arm64-v8a` \\u2014 modern 64-bit phones (most phones since ~2019)\\n- `app-armeabi-v7a` \\u2014 older 32-bit phones\\n- `app-x86` / `app-x86_64` \\u2014 emulators / some tablets & Chromebooks\\n- `app-universal` \\u2014 works on all of the above, just a bit larger\\n\\n## 1. Point it at your website\\n\\nOpen `capacitor.config.json` and either:\\n\\n**A) Load your live website (simplest)** \\u2014 add a `server.url`:\\n```json\\n{\\n  \\"appId\\": \\"com.example.mywebapp\\",\\n  \\"appName\\": \\"My Web App\\",\\n  \\"webDir\\": \\"www\\",\\n  \\"server\\": {\\n    \\"url\\": \\"https://your-website.com\\",\\n    \\"androidScheme\\": \\"https\\"\\n  }\\n}\\n```\\n\\n**B) Bundle static files offline** \\u2014 delete everything in `www/` and put your built\\nwebsite\'s files there instead (the `index.html`, `css/`, `js/`, etc.), and remove\\n`server.url` if present. The app will work without an internet connection.\\n\\nAlso update `appId` (a unique reverse-domain ID, e.g. `com.yourcompany.appname`) and\\n`appName` (the name shown under the icon).\\n\\n## 2. Push to GitHub\\n\\nCreate a repo and push this whole folder to it. The workflow at\\n`.github/workflows/build.yml` runs automatically on every push to `main`.\\n\\n## 3. Get your APK\\n\\nGo to your repo\'s **Actions** tab \\u2192 click the latest run \\u2192 scroll to **Artifacts** \\u2192\\ndownload `app-debug-apks` (easiest to just install and test) or `app-release-apks`.\\nUnzip it, pick the APK matching the device (or the universal one), transfer it to\\nyour phone, and install it (you\'ll need to allow \\"install from unknown sources\\" once).\\n\\nYou can also trigger a build manually anytime from the Actions tab \\u2192 **Build APK (all\\ndevices)** \\u2192 **Run workflow**.\\n\\n## Signing for production (Play Store)\\n\\nThe release APKs in this workflow are signed with the Android **debug key**, so they\\ninstall fine for testing but Google Play will reject them. To publish for real:\\n\\n1. Generate a keystore: `keytool -genkey -v -keystore release.keystore -alias my-key -keyalg RSA -keysize 2048 -validity 10000`\\n2. Add it as a GitHub Actions secret (base64-encode the file) along with the store/key passwords.\\n3. In `android/app/build.gradle`, add a real `signingConfigs.release` block using those secrets and reference it from `buildTypes.release.signingConfig` instead of `signingConfigs.debug`.\\n4. Update `build.yml` to decode the keystore secret into a file before the release build step.\\n\\nHappy to wire this up for you if/when you have a keystore ready.\\n\\n## App icon & splash screen\\n\\nCapacitor uses default placeholder icons. To customize:\\n```\\nnpm install @capacitor/assets --save-dev\\nnpx capacitor-assets generate\\n```\\n(after placing your `icon.png` / `splash.png` source images per the\\n[@capacitor/assets docs](https://github.com/ionic-team/capacitor-assets)).\\n\\n## Project structure\\n```\\ncapacitor.config.json     # points the app at your website (or bundled files)\\nwww/                      # bundled web files (only used if server.url is not set)\\nandroid/                  # native Android project (generated, safe to regenerate via `npx cap add android`)\\n.github/workflows/build.yml   # The project is now a\\nfull cross-platform mobile-app factory (Android + iOS), plugin-rich,\\nPWA-ready, offline-capable, and fully automated via GitHub Actions.\\n\\n### Added\\n\\n#### Web layer (PWA + offline)\\n- Full PWA `www/index.html` with theme color, viewport, Apple touch icons,\\n  manifest link, and Open Graph / Twitter card meta tags.\\n- `manifest.json` with name, short name, icons, theme/background colors,\\n  display mode (`standalone`), orientation, scope, start URL, and app\\n  shortcuts.\\n- Service worker (`sw.js`) with a cache-first strategy for the shell and a\\n  network-first strategy for navigations, plus an offline fallback page.\\n- `offline.html` fallback shown when the device is offline.\\n- `app.js` — Capacitor plugin bridge (status bar, splash, haptics, network,\\n  app lifecycle, back-button handling, pull-to-refresh, deep-link listener).\\n- `styles.css` — system-font stack, safe-area insets, dark-mode awareness,\\n  and a styled offline page.\\n- `robots.txt`, `humans.txt`, `browserconfig.xml`, and an OG image asset.\\n\\n#### Capacitor plugins (18 added)\\n- `@capacitor/status-bar` — themed status bar that does not overlay WebView.\\n- `@capacitor/splash-screen` — configurable launch splash with fade-out.\\n- `@capacitor/haptics` — native haptic feedback.\\n- `@capacitor/app` — lifecycle, back button, app state, URL open.\\n- `@capacitor/keyboard` — keyboard resize & style.\\n- `@capacitor/preferences` — key/value persistent storage.\\n- `@capacitor/network` — online/offline detection + listener.\\n- Every push\\nto `main` produces per-architecture Android APKs, a release AAB (Play Store\\nbundle), and an iOS Simulator build, all as downloadable artifacts.\\n\\n> **What\'s new in v2.0?** This is a ground-up upgrade: 18 Capacitor plugins,\\n> a full PWA + offline service worker, hardened Android/iOS native configs,\\n> signed release builds, a multi-workflow CI/CD pipeline, Dependabot, a\\n> one-command setup, and comprehensive docs. See the\\n> [Changelog](CHANGELOG.md) for the full breakdown.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## ✨ Features\\n\\n- **Cross-platform** — one codebase ships to Android (APK + AAB) and iOS.\\n- **Two modes** — load a live website via `server.url`, **or** bundle static\\n  files for full offline use.\\n- **PWA-ready** — manifest, theme color, maskable icons, and a service worker\\n  with an offline fallback page, all included out of the box.\\n- **18 Capacitor plugins** pre-wired — status bar, splash screen, haptics,\\n  app lifecycle, keyboard, network, geolocation, camera, file system, share,\\n  push & local notifications, device info, browser, toast, clipboard, and\\n  preferences. See [PLUGINS.md](PLUGINS.md).\\n- **Per-architecture APKs + universal** — `arm64-v8a`, `armeabi-v7a`, `x86`,\\n  `x86_64`, plus a `universal` APK that works everywhere.\\n- **Release AAB** — Play Store-ready Android App Bundle, in addition to APKs.\\n- **Signed release builds** — driven by GitHub Actions secrets; falls back to\\n  the debug key locally so it always builds out of the box.\\n- **Hardened native configs** — HTTPS-enforcing network security config,\\n  scoped backup rules, deep-link intent filters, ATS, and all required iOS\\n  permission usage strings.\\n- **Automated CI/CD** — `build.yml` (matrix build), `release.yml` (tagged\\n  releases → GitHub Releases), `lint.yml` (quality gates), and Dependabot.\\n- **One-command setup** — `make setup` or `scripts/setup.sh`.\\n- **Custom icons & splash** — generate native icon/splash assets from two\\n  source images via `@capacitor/assets`.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## 📦 Table of contents\\n\\n1. [Quick start](#-quick-start)\\n2. [How it works](#-how-it-works)\\n3. [Configuration](#-configuration)\\n4. [Build locally](#-build-locally)\\n5. [CI/CD](#-cicd)\\n6. [Signing for production](#-signing-for-production-play-store)\\n7. [Publishing](#-publishing)\\n8. [App icon & splash screen](#-app-icon--splash-screen)\\n9. [Plugins](#-plugins)\\n10. [Project structure](#-project-structure)\\n11. [Troubleshooting](#-troubleshooting)\\n12. [FAQ](#-faq)\\n13. [Contributing](#-contributing)\\n14. [License](#-license)\\n\\n---\\n\\n## 🚀 Quick start\\n\\n### Option A — load a live website (simplest)\\n\\n1. **Point it at your site.** Open `capacitor.config.json` and add a\\n   `server.url`:\\n\\n   ```json\\n   {\\n     \\"appId\\": \\"com.yourcompany.appname\\",\\n     \\"appName\\": \\"Your App\\",\\n     \\"webDir\\": \\"www\\",\\n     \\"server\\": {\\n       \\"url\\": \\"https://your-website.com\\",\\n       \\"androidScheme\\": \\"https\\"\\n     }\\n   }\\n   ```\\n\\n2. **Set your identity.** Update `appId` (a unique reverse-domain id, e.g.\\n   `com.yourcompany.appname`) and `appName` (shown under the icon). Also\\n   update `android/app/src/main/res/values/strings.xml`,\\n   `android/app/build.gradle` (`applicationId` / `namespace`),\\n   `android/app/src/main/AndroidManifest.xml` (`<data android:scheme>`), and\\n   the iOS `Info.plist` URL scheme + bundle id. The helper script\\n   `scripts/setup.sh` can rename these for you.\\n\\n3. **Push to GitHub.** Create a repo and push this folder. The workflow at\\n   `.github/workflows/build.yml` runs automatically on every push to `main`.\\n\\n4. **Get your app.** Go to your repo\'s **Actions** tab → click the latest\\n   run → scroll to **Artifacts** → download `app-debug-apks` (easiest to\\n   install and test), `app-release-apks`, `app-release-aab`, or\\n   `app-ios-simulator`. Unzip, pick the APK matching your device (or the\\n   universal one), transfer it to your phone, and install it (allow\\n   \\"install from unknown sources\\" once).\\n\\n### Option B — bundle static files (offline)\\n\\n1. Delete the contents of `www/` and put your built website\'s files there\\n   instead (`index.html`, `css/`, `js/`, `assets/`, etc.). Make sure\\n   `server.url` is **not** set in `capacitor.config.json`. The app will then\\n   work without an internet connection — the included service worker caches\\n   the app shell and serves an offline fallback page when needed.\\n\\n2. Follow steps 2–4 above.\\n\\n> **Tip:** You can keep the bundled `www/` PWA shell as a starting point and\\n> just drop your own `index.html` and assets in. The service worker, manifest,\\n> and Capacitor bridge in `app.js` are ready to use.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## 🧠 How it works\\n\\nFor the full architecture, see [ARCHITECTURE.md](ARCHITECTURE.md). The short\\nversion:\\n\\n1. **`capacitor.config.json`** is the single source of truth — it tells\\n   Capacitor the `appId`, `appName`, where the web files live (`webDir`),\\n   and (optionally) the live `server.url` to load.\\n2. **`www/`** holds the web content. Either your bundled static site or the\\n   included PWA placeholder (ignored when `server.url` is set).\\n3. **`android/`** and **`ios/`** are native shell projects Capacitor\\n   generates. `npx cap sync` copies `www/` into them and wires up plugins.\\n4. **GitHub Actions** run `npm ci` → `npx cap sync` → Gradle/Xcode build →\\n   upload artifacts (and, for tags, publish a Release).\\n\\n```\\n            ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────┐\\n            │            capacitor.config.json             │\\n            │   appId / appName / webDir / server.url      │\\n            └───────────────┬──────────────────────────────┘\\n                            │  npx cap sync\\n            ┌───────────────▼──────────────┐\\n            │            www/              │   (your site or bundled PWA)\\n            │  index.html, css, js, sw.js  │\\n            └───────────────┬──────────────┘\\n                            │\\n        ┌───────────────────┴───────────────────┐\\n        ▼                                       ▼\\n  ┌───────────┐  Gradle assembleDebug/   ┌───────────┐  xcodebuild\\n  │  android/ │  assembleRelease/        │   ios/    │  (simulator)\\n  │  (APK/AAB)│  bundleRelease           │  (.app)   │\\n  └─────┬─────┘                          └─────┬─────┘\\n        └───────────────┬──────────────────────┘\\n                        ▼\\n              GitHub Actions artifacts\\n              (debug/release APKs, AAB, .app)\\n                        │\\n                        ▼ (on tag push)\\n                 GitHub Release\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n## ⚙️ Configuration\\n\\nEverything is documented in [CONFIGURATION.md](CONFIGURATION.md). The key\\nfields in `capacitor.config.json`:\\n\\n| Field | Required | Description |\\n|-------|----------|-------------|\\n| `appId` | ✅ | Unique reverse-domain id, e.g. `com.yourcompany.appname`. |\\n| `appName` | ✅ | Human-readable name shown under the app icon. |\\n| `webDir` | ✅ | Folder with your web files (default `www`). |\\n| `server.url` | — | Load a live website instead of bundled files. |\\n| `server.androidScheme` | — | `https` (default) or `http`. |\\n| `server.cleartext` | — | Allow HTTP traffic (not recommended). |\\n| `loggingBehavior` | — | `debug` / `production` / `none`. |\\n| `plugins` | — | Per-plugin config (splash, status bar, keyboard, …). |\\n\\n---\\n\\n## 🔨 Build locally\\n\\nPrerequisites: Node 22, JDK 21 (Android), Xcode (iOS, macOS only).\\n\\n```bash\\n# 1. Install deps\\nnpm install\\n\\n# 2. Sync web assets into native projects\\nnpm run sync            # both platforms\\nnpm run sync:android    # Android only\\nnpm run sync:ios        # iOS only\\n\\n# 3a. Build Android\\nnpm run build:apk       # debug APKs\\nnpm run build:release   # release APKs (debug-signed locally)\\nnpm run build:bundle    # release AAB\\n\\n# 3b. Build iOS (macOS only, simulator)\\nnpm run build:ios\\n\\n# 4. Open in the native IDE\\nnpm run open:android    # opens Android Studio\\nnpm run open:ios        # opens Xcode\\n```\\n\\nThere\'s also a **Makefile** with the same targets (`make build-apk`,\\n`make build-release`, `make sync`, `make assets`, …) and convenience\\nshell scripts in `scripts/` (see [Section 8](#-tooling)).\\n\\n---\\n\\n## 🤖 CI/CD\\n\\nThree workflows live in `.github/workflows/`:\\n\\n### `build.yml` — continuous build\\nRuns on every push/PR to `main` and via manual dispatch. A `validate` job\\nchecks the config and structure first, then a **matrix** builds three Android\\nvariants (`debug`, `release`, `bundle`) in parallel plus an iOS Simulator\\nbuild. Artifacts: `app-debug-apks`, `app-release-apks`, `app-release-aab`,\\n`app-ios-simulator`.\\n\\n### `release.yml` — tagged releases\\nTriggered by pushing a tag like `v1.2.0`. Decodes a signing keystore from the\\n`KEYSTORE_BASE64` secret (if present), builds signed release APKs + AAB, and\\ncreates a **GitHub Release** with auto-generated changelog notes and the\\nartifacts attached.\\n\\n```bash\\ngit tag v1.2.0\\ngit push origin v1.2.0   # → triggers release.yml\\n```\\n\\n### `lint.yml` — quality gates\\nRuns Prettier, markdownlint, JSON syntax checks, and the config validator on\\nevery PR.\\n\\n### Dependabot\\n`.github/dependabot.yml` opens weekly PRs to bump npm packages, GitHub\\nActions, and Gradle dependencies.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## 🔐 Signing for production (Play Store)\\n\\nThe release APKs/AAB built by `build.yml` are signed with the Android\\n**debug key**, so they install fine for testing but Google Play will reject\\nthem. To publish for real:\\n\\n1. **Generate a keystore:**\\n   ```bash\\n   keytool -genkey -v -keystore release.keystore -alias my-key \\\\\\n     -keyalg RSA -keysize 2048 -validity 10000\\n   ```\\n\\n2. **Add GitHub Actions secrets** to your repo (Settings → Secrets and\\n   variables → Actions):\\n   - `KEYSTORE_BASE64` — `base64 -w0 release.keystore` (the file contents)\\n   - `KEYSTORE_PASSWORD` — the keystore password\\n   - `KEY_ALIAS` — e.g. `my-key`\\n   - `KEY_PASSWORD` — the key password\\n\\n3. The `release.yml` workflow decodes `KEYSTORE_BASE64` to a temp file and\\n   `build.gradle` reads `KEYSTORE_PATH` / `KEYSTORE_PASSWORD` / `KEY_ALIAS` /\\n   `KEY_PASSWORD` from the environment. Push a tag and you\'ll get a signed\\n   GitHub Release.\\n\\n> Locally, release builds automatically fall back to the debug signing config\\n> so they always work without secrets.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## 📲 Publishing\\n\\nSee [PUBLISHING.md](PUBLISHING.md) for illustrated, step-by-step guides:\\n\\n- **Google Play Store** — create a developer account, set up your app, upload\\n  the signed AAB, fill the store listing, and roll out.\\n- **Apple App Store** — enroll in the Apple Developer Program, configure\\n  signing & capabilities, archive in Xcode, and submit via App Store Connect.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## 🎨 App icon & splash screen\\n\\nSource images live in `resources/`:\\n\\n- `resources/icon/icon.png` — at least 1024×1024 PNG (the app icon source).\\n- `resources/splash/splash.png` — at least 2732×2732 PNG (the splash source).\\n\\nGenerate all native sizes (Android mipmaps, iOS asset catalog) with:\\n\\n```bash\\nnpm run assets            # both platforms\\nnpm run assets:android    # Android only\\nnpm run assets:ios        # iOS only\\n# or\\nmake assets\\n# or\\nscripts/generate-icons.sh\\n```\\n\\nThis uses [`@capacitor/assets`](https://github.com/ionic-team/capacitor-assets).\\nThe PWA icons in `www/assets/` (192, 512, maskable, favicon) are already\\ngenerated and referenced by `manifest.json`.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## 🧩 Plugins\\n\\n18 official Capacitor plugins are installed and configured. The JS bridge in\\n`www/app.js` initializes status bar, splash, keyboard, network, app lifecycle,\\nback button, and haptics for you. See [PLUGINS.md](PLUGINS.md) for the full\\nlist with code examples. Quick taste:\\n\\n```js\\nimport { Haptics } from \'@capacitor/haptics\';\\nawait Haptics.impact({ style: \'medium\' });\\n\\nimport { Share } from \'@capacitor/share\';\\nawait Share.share({ title: \'Check this out\', url: \'https://example.com\' });\\n```\\n\\nTo add a new plugin: `npm install @capacitor/<plugin>` → `npm run sync` →\\nadd any config to `capacitor.config.json` → document it in `PLUGINS.md` →\\nadd native permissions if needed.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## 🗂️ Project structure\\n\\n```\\ncapacitor.config.json     # single source of app config (appId, name, plugins)\\nwww/                      # web assets (your site or bundled PWA)\\n  index.html              # PWA shell / placeholder\\n  manifest.json           # PWA manifest\\n  sw.js                   # service worker (offline)\\n  app.js                  # Capacitor plugin bridge\\n  styles.css              # default styles\\n  offline.html            # offline fallback page\\n  assets/                 # icons, favicon, og image\\nandroid/                  # native Android project\\nios/                      # native iOS project\\nresources/                # source icon & splash images\\n.github/workflows/        # CI/CD: build, release, lint\\n.github/dependabot.yml    # automated dependency updates\\nscripts/                  # helper shell + node scripts\\ndocs/                     # long-form documentation\\nMakefile                  # convenience targets\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n## 🛠️ Troubleshooting\\n\\nSee [TROUBLESHOOTING.md](TROUBLESHOOTING.md) for common issues. Highlights:\\n\\n- **Blank screen / site won\'t load** — check `server.url` and your site\'s\\n  CSP / HTTPS. For HTTP sites, set `server.cleartext: true` (not recommended).\\n- **\\"Install blocked\\"** on Android — enable \\"Install unknown apps\\" for your\\n  browser/file manager.\\n- **Release APK rejected by Play** — it\'s debug-signed. Set up a real\\n  keystore and secrets (see [Signing](#-signing-for-production-play-store)).\\n- **iOS build fails** — run `npx cap sync ios` and, on macOS, `pod install`\\n  inside `ios/App`.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## ❓ FAQ\\n\\n<details>\\n<summary><b>Do I need Android Studio / Xcode installed?</b></summary>\\n\\nNo. GitHub Actions builds everything in the cloud. You only need them\\nlocally if you want to run on a device from your machine or tweak native\\ncode.\\n</details>\\n\\n<details>\\n<summary><b>Can I publish to the Play Store / App Store?</b></summary>\\n\\nYes. The release AAB/APKs are Play-ready once you sign with a real keystore\\n(see [Signing](#-signing-for-production-play-store)). For iOS you need an\\nApple Developer account and code signing — see [PUBLISHING.md](PUBLISHING.md).\\n</details>\\n\\n<details>\\n<summary><b>Will my site work offline?</b></summary>\\n\\nIf you bundle your files in `www/`, yes — fully offline. The included\\nservice worker caches the app shell and shows `offline.html` when the\\nnetwork is down. If you use `server.url`, offline behaviour depends on your\\nwebsite\'s own caching.\\n</details>\\n\\n<details>\\n<summary><b>How do I add push notifications?</b></summary>\\n\\n`@capacitor/push-notifications` is already included. Configure Firebase\\n(`google-services.json` for Android, `GoogleService-Info.plist` for iOS),\\nadd them to the project (they\'re git-ignored), and call the plugin from JS.\\nSee [PLUGINS.md](PLUGINS.md).\\n</details>\\n\\n<details>\\n<summary><b>How do I change the app id / name everywhere at once?</b></summary>\\n\\nRun `scripts/setup.sh` which rewrites the placeholder `com.example.mywebapp`\\n/ \\"My Web App\\" across the Android and iOS native files for you.\\n</details>\\n\\n---\\n\\n## 🤝 Contributing\\n\\nContributions are welcome! Must match the Android `applicationId`/`namespace` and the iOS bundle id. |\\n| `appName` | string | — | **Required.** Human-readable name shown under the app icon and in the app switcher. |\\n| `webDir` | string | `www` | Directory (relative to the project root) containing the web files to bundle. |\\n| `bundledWebRuntime` | boolean | `false` | Legacy Capacitor 2 option. Leave `false` for Capacitor 3+. |\\n| `androidScheme` | string | `https` | Scheme used to serve bundled files on Android (`https` or `http`). `https` is recommended. |\\n| `iosScheme` | string | `capacitor` | Scheme used to serve bundled files on iOS (`capacitor` or `http`). |\\n| `loggingBehavior` | string | `debug` | One of `debug`, `production`, `none`. Controls native console log verbosity. |\\n| `errorReporting` | boolean | `true` | When `true`, the native bridge surfaces uncaught JS errors to the system log. |\\n\\n### `server`\\n\\nControls how the WebView loads content.\\n\\n```json\\n\\"server\\": {\\n  \\"androidScheme\\": \\"https\\",\\n  \\"iosScheme\\": \\"capacitor\\",\\n  \\"cleartext\\": false,\\n  \\"url\\": \\"https://your-website.com\\"\\n}\\n```\\n\\n| Field | Type | Default | Description |\\n|-------|------|---------|-------------|\\n| `url` | string | — | **Optional.** If set, the app loads this live website instead of the bundled `www/` files. Great for sites that already work well on mobile. |\\n| `androidScheme` | string | `https` | Overrides the top-level scheme for Android. |\\n| `iosScheme` | string | `capacitor` | Overrides the top-level scheme for iOS. |\\n| `cleartext` | boolean | `false` | Allow plain HTTP traffic. Only set `true` if you must load an `http://` site (e.g. local dev). |\\n\\n### `plugins`\\n\\nPer-plugin configuration blocks. Only plugins you\'ve installed and synced\\nread these. The defaults shipped here:\\n\\n#### `SplashScreen`\\n\\n```json\\n\\"SplashScreen\\": {\\n  \\"launchShowDuration\\": 2000,\\n  \\"launchAutoHide\\": true,\\n  \\"backgroundColor\\": \\"#0b1020\\",\\n  \\"androidSplashResourceName\\": \\"splash\\",\\n  \\"androidScaleType\\": \\"CENTER_CROP\\",\\n  \\"showSpinner\\": false,\\n  \\"iosSpinnerStyle\\": \\"small\\",\\n  \\"splashFullScreen\\": true,\\n  \\"splashImmersive\\": true,\\n  \\"fadeOutDuration\\": 400\\n}\\n```\\n\\n`launchShowDuration` is how long the native splash stays before the JS calls\\n`SplashScreen.hide()` (or it auto-hides). `backgroundColor` should match\\nyour splash image background for a seamless transition.\\n\\n#### `StatusBar`\\n\\n```json\\n\\"StatusBar\\": {\\n  \\"style\\": \\"DARK\\",\\n  \\"backgroundColor\\": \\"#0b1020\\",\\n  \\"overlaysWebView\\": false,\\n  \\"iosAnimation\\": true\\n}\\n```\\n\\n`style` is `DARK` (light icons on dark) or `LIGHT` (dark icons on light) on\\niOS; on Android it sets the background color. `overlaysWebView: false` means\\nthe WebView starts below the status bar — recommended for predictable layout.\\n\\n#### `Keyboard`\\n\\n```json\\n\\"Keyboard\\": {\\n  \\"resize\\": \\"body\\",\\n  \\"style\\": \\"DARK\\",\\n  \\"resizeOnFullScreen\\": true\\n}\\n```\\n\\n`resize` controls how the app reacts to the keyboard: `body` (resize the\\npage), `native` (resize the WebView), `ionic` (for Ionic apps), or `none`.\\n\\n#### `CapacitorHttp`\\n\\n```json\\n\\"CapacitorHttp\\": {\\n  \\"enabled\\": true\\n}\\n```\\n\\nWhen enabled, `fetch`/`XHR` are routed through the native HTTP stack,\\n**bypassing CORS** and using native cookies/certificates. Useful when your\\nweb code hits APIs that don\'t send CORS headers.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Keeping native files in sync\\n\\nWhen you change `appId` / `appName`, these files must also be updated:\\n\\n| File | What to change |\\n|------|----------------|\\n| `android/app/build.gradle` | `applicationId`, `namespace` |\\n| `android/app/src/main/AndroidManifest.xml` | `<data android:scheme>` deep-link scheme |\\n| `android/app/src/main/res/values/strings.xml` | `app_name`, `title_activity_main`, `package_name`, `custom_url_scheme` |\\n| `android/app/src/main/java/com/example/mywebapp/MainActivity.java` | package declaration + directory path |\\n| `ios/App/App/Info.plist` | `CFBundleDisplayName`, the `CFBundleURLSchemes` scheme |\\n| `ios/App/App/capacitor.config.json` | `appId`, `appName` |\\n| `ios/App/App.xcodeproj/project.pbxproj` | `PRODUCT_BUNDLE_IDENTIFIER` |\\n\\nThe helper script `scripts/setup.sh` automates the common renames.\\n", "file_path": "web2apk/CONFIGURATION.md"}'}}]}

The JS bridge in `www/app.js` already initializes the core ones\\n(status bar, splash, keyboard, network, app lifecycle, back button,\\nhaptics). In a\\nplain browser the calls no-op gracefully (see `www/app.js` for the pattern).\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Core UI plugins\\n\\n### `@capacitor/status-bar`\\nControl the status bar appearance and whether it overlays the WebView.\\n\\n```js\\nimport { StatusBar, Style } from \'@capacitor/status-bar\';\\n\\nawait StatusBar.setStyle({ style: Style.Dark });\\nawait StatusBar.setBackgroundColor({ color: \'#0b1020\' });\\nawait StatusBar.setOverlaysWebView({ overlay: false });\\nawait StatusBar.show();\\nawait StatusBar.hide();\\n```\\n\\n### `@capacitor/splash-screen`\\nProgrammatically show/hide the launch splash.\\n\\n```js\\nimport { SplashScreen } from \'@capacitor/splash-screen\';\\nawait SplashScreen.show({ autoHide: true, showDuration: 1000 });\\nawait SplashScreen.hide();\\n```\\n\\n### `@capacitor/keyboard`\\nKeyboard appearance and resize behaviour. (iOS only; Android uses windowSoftInputMode.)\\n\\n```js\\nimport { Keyboard } from \'@capacitor/keyboard\';\\nawait Keyboard.setResizeMode({ mode: \'body\' });\\nawait Keyboard.setStyle({ style: \'DARK\' });\\nKeyboard.addListener(\'keyboardWillShow\', (info) => console.log(info.keyboardHeight));\\n```\\n\\n### `@capacitor/haptics`\\nNative tactile feedback.\\n\\n```js\\nimport { Haptics, ImpactStyle, NotificationType } from \'@capacitor/haptics\';\\nawait Haptics.impact({ style: ImpactStyle.Medium });\\nawait Haptics.notification({ type: NotificationType.Success });\\nawait Haptics.selection();\\nawait Haptics.vibrate({ duration: 200 });\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n## App & lifecycle\\n\\n### `@capacitor/app`\\nApp state, back button, URL opens, app info.\\n\\n```js\\nimport { App } from \'@capacitor/app\';\\nApp.addListener(\'backButton\', ({ canGoBack }) => {\\n  if (canGoBack) history.back(); else App.exitApp();\\n});\\nApp.addListener(\'appStateChange\', ({ isActive }) => console.log(isActive));\\nApp.addListener(\'appUrlOpen\', ({ url }) => console.log(\'Deep link:\', url));\\nconst info = await App.getInfo();      // { id, name, build, version }\\nawait App.minimizeApp();\\n```\\n\\n### `@capacitor/device`\\nDevice hardware/OS info.\\n\\n```js\\nimport { Device } from \'@capacitor/device\';\\nconst info = await Device.getInfo();\\n// { platform, osVersion, model, manufacturer, isVirtual, ... }\\nconst battery = await Device.getBatteryInfo();\\nconst lang = await Device.getLanguageCode();\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Networking & storage\\n\\n### `@capacitor/network`\\nOnline/offline status and changes.\\n\\n```js\\nimport { Network } from \'@capacitor/network\';\\nconst status = await Network.getStatus();      // { connected, connectionType }\\nNetwork.addListener(\'networkStatusChange\', (s) => console.log(s));\\n```\\n\\n### `@capacitor/preferences`\\nPersistent key/value storage (wraps SharedPreferences / NSUserDefaults).\\n\\n```js\\nimport { Preferences } from \'@capacitor/preferences\';\\nawait Preferences.set({ key: \'theme\', value: \'dark\' });\\nconst { value } = await Preferences.get({ key: \'theme\' });\\nawait Preferences.remove({ key: \'theme\' });\\nawait Preferences.clear();\\n```\\n\\n### `@capacitor/file-system`\\nRead/write files in sandboxed directories.\\n\\n```js\\nimport { Filesystem, Directory, Encoding } from \'@capacitor/file-system\';\\nawait Filesystem.writeFile({\\n  path: \'notes.txt\', data: \'Hello\', directory: Directory.Documents, encoding: Encoding.UTF8,\\n});\\nconst res = await Filesystem.readFile({ path: \'notes.txt\', directory: Directory.Documents });\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Media & sensors\\n\\n### `@capacitor/camera`\\nTake a photo or pick from the gallery.\\n\\n```js\\nimport { Camera, CameraResultType, CameraSource } from \'@capacitor/camera\';\\nconst photo = await Camera.getPhoto({\\n  quality: 90,\\n  resultType: CameraResultType.Uri,\\n  source: CameraSource.Prompt,   // let user choose camera or gallery\\n});\\n// photo.webPath can be used in an <img src>\\n```\\n\\n### `@capacitor/geolocation`\\nDevice location.\\n\\n```js\\nimport { Geolocation } from \'@capacitor/geolocation\';\\nconst pos = await Geolocation.getCurrentPosition();\\nconst watch = await Geolocation.watchPosition({}, (p) => console.log(p));\\n// await Geolocation.clearWatch({ id: watch });\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Notifications\\n\\n### `@capacitor/local-notifications`\\nSchedule notifications without a server.\\n\\n```js\\nimport { LocalNotifications } from \'@capacitor/local-notifications\';\\nawait LocalNotifications.requestPermissions();\\nawait LocalNotifications.schedule({\\n  notifications: [{\\n    id: 1, title: \'Reminder\', body: \'Time to go!\',\\n    schedule: { at: new Date(Date.now() + 60_000) },\\n  }],\\n});\\n```\\n\\n### `@capacitor/push-notifications`\\nRemote push via Firebase (Android) / APNs (iOS).\\n\\n```js\\nimport { PushNotifications, Token } from \'@capacitor/push-notifications\';\\nawait PushNotifications.requestPermissions();\\nawait PushNotifications.register();\\nPushNotifications.addListener(\'registration\', ({ value }) => console.log(\'token\', value));\\nPushNotifications.addListener(\'pushNotificationReceived\', (n) => console.log(n));\\n```\\n\\n> **Setup:** drop `google-services.json` into `android/app/` and\\n> `GoogleService-Info.plist` into `ios/App/App/` (both git-ignored). See the\\n> [Capacitor push guide](https://capacitorjs.com/docs/apis/push-notifications).\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Sharing & utilities\\n\\n### `@capacitor/share`\\nNative share sheet.\\n\\n```js\\nimport { Share } from \'@capacitor/share\';\\nawait Share.share({ title: \'Look\', text: \'Cool app\', url: \'https://example.com\', dialogTitle: \'Share via\' });\\n```\\n\\n### `@capacitor/browser`\\nIn-app browser for external links (keeps users in your app).\\n\\n```js\\nimport { Browser } from \'@capacitor/browser\';\\nawait Browser.open({ url: \'https://capacitorjs.com\' });\\nawait Browser.close();\\n```\\n\\n### `@capacitor/toast`\\nNative toast messages.\\n\\n```js\\nimport { Toast } from \'@capacitor/toast\';\\nawait Toast.show({ text: \'Saved!\', duration: \'short\' });\\n```\\n\\n### `@capacitor/clipboard`\\nRead/write the system clipboard.\\n\\n```js\\nimport { Clipboard } from \'@capacitor/clipboard\';\\nawait Clipboard.write({ string: \'copied text\' });\\nconst { value } = await Clipboard.read();\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Adding a new plugin\\n\\n1. `npm install @capacitor/<plugin>`\\n2. `npm run sync` (copies the plugin into `android/` and `ios/`)\\n3. If it needs config, add a block under `plugins` in `capacitor.config.json`\\n4. Add native permissions + usage strings:\\n   - Android: `android/app/src/main/AndroidManifest.xml`\\n   - iOS: `ios/App/App/Info.plist` (a `NS<Feature>UsageDescription` key)\\n5. Document it in this file and call it from your app code.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Notes\\n\\n- Plugins that need a native dependency (e.g. Firebase) may require adding a\\n  config file (`google-services.json` / `GoogleService-Info.plist`) and\\n  running `pod install` in `ios/App` on macOS.\\n- The `registerPlugin(name)` helper in `www/app.js` is the framework-agnostic\\n  way to grab a plugin instance without a bundler. With a bundler, prefer\\n  the named imports shown above.\\n", "file_path": "web2apk/PLUGINS.md"}'}}]}

Architecture\\n\\nThis document explains how the pieces of web2apk fit together, from your\\nwebsite to a published app.\\n\\n## High-level data flow\\n\\n```\\n┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐\\n│                          Your website                                  │\\n│  (either a live URL via server.url, OR built static files in www/)     │\\n└───────────────────────────┬────────────────────────────────────────────┘\\n                            │\\n                            ▼\\n┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐\\n│                     capacitor.config.json                              │\\n│   appId · appName · webDir · server.url · plugins                      │\\n└───────────────────────────┬────────────────────────────────────────────┘\\n                            │  npx cap sync\\n                            │  (copies www/ into native projects, wires plugins)\\n          ┌─────────────────┴─────────────────┐\\n          ▼                                   ▼\\n┌─────────────────────┐               ┌─────────────────────┐\\n│      android/        │               │        ios/          │\\n│  Capacitor bridge    │               │  Capacitor bridge    │\\n│  + 18 plugins        │               │  + 18 plugins        │\\n│  + WebView           │               │  + WKWebView         │\\n└──────────┬───────────┘               └──────────┬───────────┘\\n           │ Gradle                                │ xcodebuild\\n           │ assembleDebug/Release/Bundle          │ (simulator/device)\\n           ▼                                       ▼\\n   .apk (per ABI + universal)              .app (simulator)\\n   .aab (Play bundle)                      .ipa (device, signed)\\n           │                                       │\\n           └───────────────────┬───────────────────┘\\n                               ▼\\n                  ┌────────────────────────┐\\n                  │   GitHub Actions CI    │\\n                  │  build / release / lint│\\n                  └────────────┬───────────┘\\n                               ▼\\n              Artifacts (PR) / GitHub Release (tag)\\n                               ▼\\n                 Play Store (AAB) / App Store (IPA)\\n```\\n\\n## The Capacitor runtime model\\n\\nCapacitor is a cross-platform native runtime that lets web apps access\\nnative device features. At runtime:\\n\\n1. The native app starts and shows the **splash screen** (configured in\\n   `capacitor.config.json` → `plugins.SplashScreen`).\\n2. A native **WebView** (`android.webkit.WebView` on Android, `WKWebView` on\\n   iOS) loads the bundled `www/` files — or, if `server.url` is set, your\\n   live website.\\n3. A **JavaScript bridge** (`window.Capacitor`) is injected into the WebView.\\n   Each plugin registers itself, so calling e.g. `StatusBar.setStyle(...)`\\n   from JS sends a message over the bridge to native code, which performs\\n   the action and resolves the returned Promise.\\n4. `www/app.js` runs on load and uses that bridge to set up the status bar,\\n   hide the splash, listen for network changes, handle the back button, and\\n   wire deep links.\\n\\n## Web layer responsibilities\\n\\n| File | Role |\\n|------|------|\\n| `index.html` | PWA shell / placeholder; loads CSS, registers the service worker, boots `app.js`. |\\n| `manifest.json` | PWA manifest — name, icons, theme, display mode, shortcuts. Lets the app be \\"installed\\" to the home screen in a browser too. |\\n| `sw.js` | Service worker. Precaches the app shell, network-first navigations with offline fallback, stale-while-revalidate for other assets. |\\n| `offline.html` | Shown by the service worker when a navigation fails and nothing is cached. |\\n| `app.js` | Capacitor plugin bridge + UX niceties (status bar, splash, network status card, back button, pull-to-refresh, haptics). |\\n| `styles.css` | Default dark/light theme with safe-area insets. |\\n\\n## Native layer responsibilities\\n\\n### Android\\n- `AndroidManifest.xml` declares the `MainActivity`, permissions (internet,\\n  location, camera, notifications, vibration), the `FileProvider` for\\n  sharing files, and deep-link intent filters.\\n- `MainActivity.java` extends `BridgeActivity`; plugins are registered via\\n  `capacitor.build.gradle` (generated from `capacitor.settings.gradle`).\\n- `build.gradle` handles versioning, ABI splits, release signing (env-driven\\n  with debug fallback), and R8/minify for release.\\n- `network_security_config.xml` enforces HTTPS; `backup_rules.xml` /\\n  `data_extraction_rules.xml` scope cloud backups.\\n\\n### iOS\\n- `Info.plist` declares permission usage strings (required by App Review),\\n  ATS, the custom URL scheme, orientations, and launch screen.\\n- `AppDelegate.swift` / `SceneDelegate.swift` set up the\\n  `CAPBridgeViewController` and forward deep links and push callbacks.\\n- `capacitor.config.json` (iOS) lists the `packageClassList` of plugins.\\n- `Podfile` pulls the plugin pods from `node_modules`.\\n\\n## CI/CD pipeline\\n\\n| Workflow | Trigger | Output |\\n|----------|---------|--------|\\n| `build.yml` | push/PR to `main`, manual | validate → matrix Android (debug/release/bundle APKs+AAB) + iOS sim `.app`, as artifacts |\\n| `release.yml` | tag `v*` | signed release APKs + AAB, published as a GitHub Release with changelog |\\n| `lint.yml` | push/PR to `main` | Prettier, markdownlint, JSON validity, config validation |\\n\\nCaches (Gradle, npm) speed up rebuilds; `permissions: contents: read`\\nfollows least-privilege; `concurrency` cancels superseded runs.\\n\\n## Decisions & trade-offs\\n\\n- **Capacitor (not Cordova)** — modern, maintained, first-class TypeScript\\n  plugins, simpler native projects.\\n- **Per-ABI APKs + universal** — smaller downloads for real devices while a\\n  universal APK covers edge cases; an AAB is produced for Play Store.\\n- **Service worker by default** — gives bundled apps instant offline\\n  support; harmless for `server.url` apps (the SW only caches same-origin\\n  shell assets).\\n- **Debug-signed releases by default** — keeps the workflow zero-config; real\\n  signing is opt-in via secrets.\\n", "file_path": "web2apk/ARCHITECTURE.md"}'}}]}

Use the\\n  `app-release-aab` artifact (or `npm run build:bundle`).\\n- The default release build is **debug-signed**. Set up a real keystore and\\n  the `KEYSTORE_*` secrets (see\\n  [README → Signing](README.md#-signing-for-production-play-store)).\\n\\n### Build fails in CI with \\"SDK licence not accepted\\"\\n- The `android-actions/setup-android@v3` action accepts licences\\n  automatically. If it fails, ensure no `local.properties` is committed\\n  (it\'s git-ignored) and that `ANDROID_SDK_ROOT` isn\'t being overridden.\\n\\n### `minifyEnabled` stripped something it shouldn\'t\\n- Add keep rules in `android/app/proguard-rules.pro` for any reflected model\\n  classes. The shipped rules already preserve Capacitor\'s JS bridge.\\n\\n## iOS\\n\\n### Build fails with \\"module \'Capacitor\' not found\\"\\n- Run `npx cap sync ios`, then (on macOS) `cd ios/App && pod install`.\\n- Open `App.xcworkspace` (not `App.xcodeproj`) in Xcode.\\n\\n### Missing usage description strings\\n- Apple requires an `NS<Feature>UsageDescription` for any permission. They\'re\\n  set in `ios/App/App/Info.plist` already; edit the text to match your app\'s\\n  actual purpose or App Review will reject it.\\n\\n### \\"No profiles for … were found\\"\\n- You\'re building for a device without signing. Enroll in the Apple\\n  Developer Program, set a Team in Xcode → Signing & Capabilities, and let\\n  Xcode auto-manage signing. The CI simulator build needs none of this.\\n\\n### Splash shows then white flash\\n- Set `plugins.SplashScreen.backgroundColor` to match your splash image, and\\n  ensure `launchShowDuration` covers the WebView load. `app.js` calls\\n  `SplashScreen.hide()` once the DOM is ready.\\n\\n## Web / PWA\\n\\n### Service worker not updating\\n- Bump `VERSION` in `www/sw.js`; the activate handler purges old caches.\\n  In development, use Chrome DevTools → Application → Service Workers →\\n  \\"Update on reload\\".\\n\\n### Icons look wrong when \\"installed\\"\\n- Make sure `manifest.json` references the maskable variants\\n  (`icon-maskable-192.png`, `icon-maskable-512.png`) with\\n  `\\"purpose\\": \\"maskable\\"`. They\'re included by default.\\n\\n### `app.js` errors \\"plugin unavailable\\"\\n- That\'s expected in a plain browser — the bridge no-ops. It only means\\n  something inside a native shell. Make sure you ran `npx cap sync` after\\n  adding a plugin.\\n\\n## General\\n\\n### How do I change the app id / name everywhere?\\n- Run `scripts/setup.sh` — it rewrites the placeholder\\n  `com.example.mywebapp` / \\"My Web App\\" across Android and iOS files. Then\\n  `npx cap sync`.\\n\\n### `npm ci` fails\\n- Delete `node_modules` and `package-lock.json`, then `npm install` to\\n  regenerate the lockfile. Ensure you\'re on Node 22 (`nvm use`).\\n\\n### Still stuck?\\n- Open an issue with the workflow run link, the exact error, and the\\n  `capacitor.config.json` (redact secrets).\\n", "file_path": "web2apk/TROUBLESHOOTING.md"}'}}]}
